## Round 3 verdict

Round 3 substantially improves the document, but it does **not** clear the elevated 9.5/10 bar.

Of the eight outstanding points:

- 4 are fully fixed.
- 2 are partially fixed.
- 2 remain materially unresolved.
- The restructuring introduces new internal inconsistencies, chiefly in §4.1 and the retroactive Wave 2 table.

### Verification of the eight points

1. **G-V1 minimum repetitions and environmental equivalence — partially fixed**

For regression/unit/integration/contract tests, the wording now requires at least two executions under defined equivalent conditions: same code, seed/fixture, and no uninjected wall clock. That fixes the literal contradiction from Round 2.

However, the performance/load/chaos branch still says the verdict “is reproducible” while its binary check requires only a predeclared threshold—not repeated executions. A threshold prevents post-hoc goalpost movement, but does not demonstrate reproducibility. Thus the two branches still use different meanings of “reproducibility.”

This inconsistency appears concretely in §5:

- W2-07 acknowledges that the load test was not rerun.
- W2-08 calls the CloudWatch transition “deterministic by construction.”
- W2-06 marks G-V1 effectively inapplicable because repetition would be expensive.

Those treatments conflict with G-V1 being declared applicable to every test/drill. G-V1 needs either:

- two equivalent runs for every category;
- a separate gate for predeclared acceptance criteria versus empirical repeatability; or
- an explicit exception rule for expensive one-shot recovery drills.

**Status: partially fixed.**

2. **G-V3’s hidden plausibility judgment — fully fixed**

G-V3 now checks only whether a concrete mutation is recorded. Its representativeness and severity are explicitly moved to weighted criterion 1.

That is a genuine binary/graded separation. A deliberately trivial mutation can pass the existence gate but receive a poor oracle-strength score, which is coherent.

**Status: fixed.**

3. **Drift with the canonical Wave 2 backlog — partially fixed**

The detailed descriptions in [pilot-readiness-program.md](/C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/engineering/pilot-readiness-program.md:130) were corrected:

- W2-05 now limits the demonstrated safety to an `occurrenceId` that never existed and explicitly says general replay deduplication remains unproved.
- W2-06 now says only RTO was measured and that RPO remains open.
- W2-07 explicitly limits the result to direct Lambda invocation rather than the complete HTTP path.

But the canonical summary table was **not** corrected:

- [W2-05](/C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/engineering/pilot-readiness-program.md:121) still says “provar que replay não duplica side effects” and remains `DONE`.
- [W2-06](/C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/engineering/pilot-readiness-program.md:122) still says “medir RPO/RTO observados” and remains `DONE`.

This directly contradicts §5’s assertion that the program was updated “to cite the narrow claims instead of repeating the original backlog description.” The detail is honest, but the top-level canonical backlog still oversells completion.

**Status: partially fixed.**

4. **Source-to-rule traceability — still incomplete**

Section 7 now maps each listed source to gates or taxonomy elements, and several citations are materially stronger:

- ISO includes the part and 2022 edition.
- Meszaros includes publisher, year, ISBN, chapter, and named patterns.
- Basiri includes journal, volume, issue, pages, year, and DOI.
- Fowler and the SRE Book include identifiable articles/chapters.

Nevertheless, the requested gate/weight-level traceability is not fully achieved:

- The ISO reference names Part 1 but no clause supporting the specific three-axis taxonomy.
- `principlesofchaos.org` is presented as a domain/title rather than a precise URL or edition/version.
- The Meszaros mapping says “Test Interdependence → G-V1 (isolamento),” but isolation is now G-V2; G-V1 is reproducibility. This is a new semantic mapping error caused by the revised gate structure.
- No source supports the numeric weights. The document honestly identifies them as project calibration, which is acceptable, but then this point is resolved by explicit non-derivation—not by the requested source-to-weight justification.

For an internal standard, named chapters and patterns can reasonably replace unstable page numbers. But the incorrect G-V1 mapping and missing clause-level ISO locator keep this from being fully traceable.

**Status: not fully fixed.**

5. **Orphaned G-V7 reference — fully fixed**

G-V2 now points to G-V5. A complete scan finds no live use of G-V7 or G-V8 as current gates; their remaining appearances are historical explanations.

**Status: fixed.**

6. **Retroactive grant of prospective G-V4/G-V5 — fixed, with a new nearby problem**

The six drills now receive `N/A retroativo` for G-V4/G-V5, and the document clearly explains why post-hoc evidence cannot prove preregistration. That resolves the exact Round 2 finding.

However, the adjacent combined column “G-V1/G-V2/G-V3” is not rigorous:

- It frequently discusses determinism “by construction” where G-V1 now requires observed reruns.
- It does not identify a written mutation record for G-V3.
- “idem W2-03” carries a determinism explanation into G-V3 even though G-V3 concerns a recorded mutation.
- W2-06 treats the universal G-V1 as inapplicable without any exception in §3.1.

The requested G-V4/G-V5 correction is real, but the table should report each applicable gate independently as `OK`, `FAIL`, `UNKNOWN/NOT EVIDENCED`, or justified `N/A`.

**Status of the original point: fixed. New internal consistency defect found.**

7. **Aggregation algorithm and N/A handling — not fixed adequately**

Section 4.1 is the largest remaining blocker. It supplies prose categories, but not an executable aggregation algorithm.

Specific defects:

- The individual-test formula lists criteria 1, 3, 5, and 6, omitting criterion 4 even though criterion 4’s declared unit is “Teste individual ou drill.” Consequently, “Evidence & honesty,” weighted at 20%, disappears from individual-test scoring.
- The suite formula says to combine criterion 2, the average of individual criteria, and criterion 6 measured for the suite, but gives no weights or equation.
- Criterion 6 is included in the individual-test average and then added again at suite level, creating apparent double counting.
- It does not say whether individual-test averages are arithmetic, weighted, case-weighted, risk-weighted, or capped by invalid tests.
- It does not define how multiple components in one PR are aggregated.
- It does not define how criteria 1, 3, 4, and 5 are aggregated across the tests contained in a report/PR.
- It says a report uses all seven criteria and has no N/A “by default,” even though a standalone operational drill may have no meaningful test-suite coverage criterion.
- The N/A rule is directionally correct—exclude from the denominator and preserve proportional weights—but no actual formula is stated.

A usable formulation would define, for each scope:

\[
score = \frac{\sum_{i \in applicable} weight_i \times criterionScore_i}{\sum_{i \in applicable} weight_i}
\]

It must then define exactly how a criterion score is derived when its unit has multiple members, such as multiple tests, claims, or components.

**Status: unresolved and blocking.**

8. **Score 10 independent of historical luck — fixed in principle**

A 10 now depends on an actual mutation check performed during evaluation, rather than on previously discovering an unknown production defect. This resolves the historical-luck problem.

There is a smaller scope ambiguity: §4.2 appears to describe overall scores, while the mutation procedure naturally applies to individual tests. It is unclear how a suite, PR, or drill report earns 10, especially when its score combines criteria that cannot themselves undergo a mutation check. This is principally a consequence of the unresolved aggregation model, not a recurrence of the historical-luck defect.

**Status of the original point: fixed.**

## New internal inconsistencies

The Round 3 hunt found no new orphaned gate number, but it did find these material issues:

1. Criterion 4 is accidentally excluded from individual-test scoring.
2. Criterion 6 appears twice in the suite calculation.
3. The suite calculation contains no numerical weighting rule.
4. The report/PR calculation does not define aggregation over multiple tests, components, or claims.
5. G-V1 is declared universal but treated as inapplicable or satisfied “by construction” in §5.
6. G-V3 is grouped with determinism commentary rather than evaluated against a written mutation record.
7. The source table maps Meszaros’s “Test Interdependence” to G-V1 as “isolation,” although isolation is G-V2.
8. §5 claims the canonical backlog descriptions were replaced, while the W2-05/W2-06 table rows retain the original broad claims.

## Independent score: **8.70/10**

Round 3 deserves a meaningful increase from 7.85. It cleanly fixes G-V3, the orphaned reference, retroactive G-V4/G-V5 treatment, and the luck-dependent score-10 anchor. It also materially improves G-V1 and canonical Wave 2 detail.

It does not reach 9.5 because the scoring mechanism—the operational heart of a calibratable standard—is still not deterministically executable. Different reviewers could follow §4.1 faithfully and produce materially different results, or omit/double-count criteria. The canonical backlog drift and the new G-V1/G-V3 inconsistencies in the retrospective audit reinforce that conclusion.

**Final status: not approved at the special 9.5/10 gate.**

55.811
## Round 3 verdict

Round 3 substantially improves the document, but it does **not** clear the elevated 9.5/10 bar.

Of the eight outstanding points:

- 4 are fully fixed.
- 2 are partially fixed.
- 2 remain materially unresolved.
- The restructuring introduces new internal inconsistencies, chiefly in §4.1 and the retroactive Wave 2 table.

### Verification of the eight points

1. **G-V1 minimum repetitions and environmental equivalence — partially fixed**

For regression/unit/integration/contract tests, the wording now requires at least two executions under defined equivalent conditions: same code, seed/fixture, and no uninjected wall clock. That fixes the literal contradiction from Round 2.

However, the performance/load/chaos branch still says the verdict “is reproducible” while its binary check requires only a predeclared threshold—not repeated executions. A threshold prevents post-hoc goalpost movement, but does not demonstrate reproducibility. Thus the two branches still use different meanings of “reproducibility.”

This inconsistency appears concretely in §5:

- W2-07 acknowledges that the load test was not rerun.
- W2-08 calls the CloudWatch transition “deterministic by construction.”
- W2-06 marks G-V1 effectively inapplicable because repetition would be expensive.

Those treatments conflict with G-V1 being declared applicable to every test/drill. G-V1 needs either:

- two equivalent runs for every category;
- a separate gate for predeclared acceptance criteria versus empirical repeatability; or
- an explicit exception rule for expensive one-shot recovery drills.

**Status: partially fixed.**

2. **G-V3’s hidden plausibility judgment — fully fixed**

G-V3 now checks only whether a concrete mutation is recorded. Its representativeness and severity are explicitly moved to weighted criterion 1.

That is a genuine binary/graded separation. A deliberately trivial mutation can pass the existence gate but receive a poor oracle-strength score, which is coherent.

**Status: fixed.**

3. **Drift with the canonical Wave 2 backlog — partially fixed**

The detailed descriptions in [pilot-readiness-program.md](/C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/engineering/pilot-readiness-program.md:130) were corrected:

- W2-05 now limits the demonstrated safety to an `occurrenceId` that never existed and explicitly says general replay deduplication remains unproved.
- W2-06 now says only RTO was measured and that RPO remains open.
- W2-07 explicitly limits the result to direct Lambda invocation rather than the complete HTTP path.

But the canonical summary table was **not** corrected:

- [W2-05](/C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/engineering/pilot-readiness-program.md:121) still says “provar que replay não duplica side effects” and remains `DONE`.
- [W2-06](/C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/engineering/pilot-readiness-program.md:122) still says “medir RPO/RTO observados” and remains `DONE`.

This directly contradicts §5’s assertion that the program was updated “to cite the narrow claims instead of repeating the original backlog description.” The detail is honest, but the top-level canonical backlog still oversells completion.

**Status: partially fixed.**

4. **Source-to-rule traceability — still incomplete**

Section 7 now maps each listed source to gates or taxonomy elements, and several citations are materially stronger:

- ISO includes the part and 2022 edition.
- Meszaros includes publisher, year, ISBN, chapter, and named patterns.
- Basiri includes journal, volume, issue, pages, year, and DOI.
- Fowler and the SRE Book include identifiable articles/chapters.

Nevertheless, the requested gate/weight-level traceability is not fully achieved:

- The ISO reference names Part 1 but no clause supporting the specific three-axis taxonomy.
- `principlesofchaos.org` is presented as a domain/title rather than a precise URL or edition/version.
- The Meszaros mapping says “Test Interdependence → G-V1 (isolamento),” but isolation is now G-V2; G-V1 is reproducibility. This is a new semantic mapping error caused by the revised gate structure.
- No source supports the numeric weights. The document honestly identifies them as project calibration, which is acceptable, but then this point is resolved by explicit non-derivation—not by the requested source-to-weight justification.

For an internal standard, named chapters and patterns can reasonably replace unstable page numbers. But the incorrect G-V1 mapping and missing clause-level ISO locator keep this from being fully traceable.

**Status: not fully fixed.**

5. **Orphaned G-V7 reference — fully fixed**

G-V2 now points to G-V5. A complete scan finds no live use of G-V7 or G-V8 as current gates; their remaining appearances are historical explanations.

**Status: fixed.**

6. **Retroactive grant of prospective G-V4/G-V5 — fixed, with a new nearby problem**

The six drills now receive `N/A retroativo` for G-V4/G-V5, and the document clearly explains why post-hoc evidence cannot prove preregistration. That resolves the exact Round 2 finding.

However, the adjacent combined column “G-V1/G-V2/G-V3” is not rigorous:

- It frequently discusses determinism “by construction” where G-V1 now requires observed reruns.
- It does not identify a written mutation record for G-V3.
- “idem W2-03” carries a determinism explanation into G-V3 even though G-V3 concerns a recorded mutation.
- W2-06 treats the universal G-V1 as inapplicable without any exception in §3.1.

The requested G-V4/G-V5 correction is real, but the table should report each applicable gate independently as `OK`, `FAIL`, `UNKNOWN/NOT EVIDENCED`, or justified `N/A`.

**Status of the original point: fixed. New internal consistency defect found.**

7. **Aggregation algorithm and N/A handling — not fixed adequately**

Section 4.1 is the largest remaining blocker. It supplies prose categories, but not an executable aggregation algorithm.

Specific defects:

- The individual-test formula lists criteria 1, 3, 5, and 6, omitting criterion 4 even though criterion 4’s declared unit is “Teste individual ou drill.” Consequently, “Evidence & honesty,” weighted at 20%, disappears from individual-test scoring.
- The suite formula says to combine criterion 2, the average of individual criteria, and criterion 6 measured for the suite, but gives no weights or equation.
- Criterion 6 is included in the individual-test average and then added again at suite level, creating apparent double counting.
- It does not say whether individual-test averages are arithmetic, weighted, case-weighted, risk-weighted, or capped by invalid tests.
- It does not define how multiple components in one PR are aggregated.
- It does not define how criteria 1, 3, 4, and 5 are aggregated across the tests contained in a report/PR.
- It says a report uses all seven criteria and has no N/A “by default,” even though a standalone operational drill may have no meaningful test-suite coverage criterion.
- The N/A rule is directionally correct—exclude from the denominator and preserve proportional weights—but no actual formula is stated.

A usable formulation would define, for each scope:

\[
score = \frac{\sum_{i \in applicable} weight_i \times criterionScore_i}{\sum_{i \in applicable} weight_i}
\]

It must then define exactly how a criterion score is derived when its unit has multiple members, such as multiple tests, claims, or components.

**Status: unresolved and blocking.**

8. **Score 10 independent of historical luck — fixed in principle**

A 10 now depends on an actual mutation check performed during evaluation, rather than on previously discovering an unknown production defect. This resolves the historical-luck problem.

There is a smaller scope ambiguity: §4.2 appears to describe overall scores, while the mutation procedure naturally applies to individual tests. It is unclear how a suite, PR, or drill report earns 10, especially when its score combines criteria that cannot themselves undergo a mutation check. This is principally a consequence of the unresolved aggregation model, not a recurrence of the historical-luck defect.

**Status of the original point: fixed.**

## Independent score: **8.70/10**
